# MCPForge — Wave 0 task backlog

**120 tasks · 15 tracks · Wave 0 = M0, the reference module made genuinely real end to end.**
Generated by Phase 4 of the planning stream, 27 Aug 2026. Companion to `docs\build-plan\04_AUTONOMY_MODEL_ROUTING_AND_TASKS.md`. Extended 27 Aug 2026 by Phase 5 (`docs\build-plan\05_GOVERNANCE_ACCESS_CONTROL_AND_CREDENTIALS.md`) with track N, `W0-HG8`, and 17 modified tasks. Modified tasks carry a `— [P5]` marker on the changed `done:` clause so the change is visible in review.

**Wave 0 delivers:** 3 module servers (`jde-fin-gl`, `jde-fin-ap`, `jde-scm-po`) · 11 tools, **6 write / 5 read** · the full manifest pipeline · the gateway · the write-safety machinery · the four discovery meta-tools · the capability probe · the SQLite state layer · the local identity layer · the portal · the benchmark harness · one Procure-to-Pay process role spanning all three servers · the consumer registry and the binding-authorization stage · consumer usage tracking and the anomaly-detector substrate · the secret store.

---

## How to read this file

Both a human and `tools\build\Invoke-ForgeBuild.ps1` read this file. Format is fixed:

```markdown
- [ ] **W0-XN** — one-line description
  - model: opus | sonnet | human
  - deps: W0-A1, W0-B2          (or: none)
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.1, 03#7.2     (document number # section)
  - touches: core/gateway/policy/**
  - done: `a command that must exit zero` and the specific thing it must prove.
```

- **The parser ignores fenced code blocks**, so the `W0-XN` template above is not a task. It would be skipped anyway: a real task id must match `^W\d+-[A-Z]+\d+$` and `XN` has no trailing digit.
- **Tick a box only when its `done:` criterion actually passed.** The lane ticks its own; a human ticks the `model: human` gates.
- **`model: human` tasks halt the lane** (exit code 11) and write `.forge-build\NEEDS-DECISION-<id>.md`. They are decision points, not failures.
- **Order matters.** The lane runs the first task whose deps are all done. Two sequencing choices are deliberate and must not be "optimised":
  1. **Track F (write safety) comes before track I (tool volume)** — Phase 1 §10.5 item 7.
  2. **The write-path portal family (J7–J9) comes before DataTable, the palette and every route** — Phase 3 §14 closing note. The least demo-able portal work is built first. This will feel wrong around week three. Leave it.
  3. **`W0-C2` carries the consumer and credential-ref audit columns before it is marked done** — Phase 5, 02 §11.3. A later migration puts a schema discontinuity in the audit hash chain, which on SQLite is the only integrity mechanism there is. Do not split this into a follow-up task.
- `reads:` uses shorthand: `01` = `01_GOALS_AND_ROADMAP.md`, `02` = `02_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `03` = `03_UX_DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`, `04` = `04_AUTONOMY_MODEL_ROUTING_AND_TASKS.md`, all in `docs\build-plan\`.

The rules that must be present and individually tested: `write:true` requires a complete `writeSafety` with a non-`none` dry-run strategy and a `reversal.class`; `reversal.class: irreversible` forces `humanApprovalRequired: true` and `reviewPath: standard`; `binding.type` in `{plsql, function}` rejects expedited review; **`identity.carries: verified` in a hand-authored manifest is rejected**; `write: true` + `binding.type: database` is rejected; `binding.ref` for `plsql` must match `^MCPFORGE_WRAP\.[A-Z0-9_]+\.[A-Z0-9_]+$`; `echoOn: write` is mandatory for every `function` write tool; sibling tools sharing an `{app}.{module}.{entity}` prefix must both carry `disambiguation`; `purpose` ≤14 words; parameter `desc` ≤12 words; enums >12 values must use `enumRef`; a `calls` field anywhere is rejected; **no string in `evals/**` may appear verbatim in any `aliases` list**. — [P5] six further rules, each with a fixture: `credentialClass: module-scoped-stored` is rejected where the probe reports `identity.carries: verified`; `credentialClass: per-user-exchanged` is rejected on `plsql`; a `bindingGrant` on `plsql` must name a wrapper package matching `^MCPFORGE_WRAP\.[A-Z0-9_]+$`; a `standingAuthorization` without a resolvable `approvalRef` and an `expiresAt` is rejected; a Consumer record with a `credential.ref` that is not a `secretRef://` URI is rejected; and the **four-part stored-credential legitimacy test** of 02 §11.5.1 is a rule that fails naming which of the four parts failed.

- [x] **W0-B10** — Wire `generated/tools/**` into the real test topology so its contract and unit tests run under a normal, committed command
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-B9, W0-B7
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#2.3, 02#7.2, 02#7.3
  - touches: vitest.config.ts, package.json, generated/**, tools/ci/**
  - done: **flagged during W0-I3/I4/I5, 8 Sep 2026** — `vitest.config.ts` currently excludes `generated/**`, and `generated/` has no `package.json`/workspace entry, so `ajv`, `ajv-formats` and `@mcpforge/shared/*` do not resolve from a generated `tool.ts`/`handler.generated.ts`/`contract.test.ts`/`unit.test.ts`. Every prior task that claimed "passes its generated contract tests" (`W0-I2`, `W0-I3`, `W0-I4`, `W0-I5`) verified this by hand-building a throwaway resolver config outside the repo and deleting it afterward — real proof the tests themselves pass, but **no committed command anyone else runs (including `forge ci`) has ever executed them.** Fix by either (a) giving `generated/` its own `package.json` and adding it to the pnpm workspace, with `ajv`/`ajv-formats`/`@mcpforge/shared` as real dependencies, each generated `contract.test.ts`/`unit.test.ts` resolving them the same way a hand-written package does; or (b) hoisting those three specifiers to root-resolvable and un-excluding `generated/**` from the root `vitest.config.ts`. Whichever is chosen must not weaken the "only `binding.custom.ts` is hand-owned" rule (02 §2.4) or the regen-diff invariant (`W0-B9`) — a `package.json` written into `generated/` is itself a generated, provenance-headed, byte-identical-on-regen artefact, not a hand-maintained one. `done:` is: `pnpm test` (root, no extra flags, no throwaway config) actually collects and runs every `generated/tools/*/contract.test.ts` and `generated/tools/*/unit.test.ts` file, `forge ci`'s existing test stage covers them without a new stage, and `forge codegen` run twice still produces a byte-identical `generated/` tree including whatever new file(s) this task adds to it. Re-verify the already-ticked `done:` clauses of `W0-I2`, `W0-I3`, `W0-I4` and `W0-I5` now execute for real under this harness — if any of the four's generated tests fail once actually wired in (as opposed to passing under the throwaway alias config), that is new information and must be reported, not silently patched away under this task's `touches:`.
  - note: this is a build-topology decision with blast radius across all 11 tools and the regen-diff gate — routed to Opus for that reason, not because any single file is individually complex.

- [x] **W0-B11** — Wire `core/portal` into the real root test topology so `pnpm test` (no `-C`, no filter) actually runs its component tests
  - model: sonnet
  - deps: W0-J4
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#7.2, 03#12.7
  - touches: vitest.config.ts, core/portal/vitest.config.ts, core/portal/tsconfig.json
  - done: **flagged during W0-J5, 8 Sep 2026, same category of gap as `W0-B10` but for a different directory.** The root `vitest.config.ts` (`vitest run`, no per-package delegation) is what `pnpm test` and `forge ci`'s test stage actually run, and it carries none of `core/portal/vitest.config.ts`'s package-local settings — no `@/*` alias, no `esbuild.jsx: 'automatic'`, no jsdom default. Confirmed live: `pnpm exec vitest run "core/portal/src/components/ui/a11y.test.tsx"` from the repo root fails outright (`Failed to resolve import "@/components/ui/dialog"`), and a plain-JSX-without-import test file fails a different way (`ReferenceError: React is not defined`) under the same root run. This means **every `core/portal` test file has been passing only when run via `pnpm -C core/portal test`**, the command W0-J1 through W0-J5 actually used and reported — a real, correct verification, but not one `pnpm test` or `forge ci` from the repo root has ever reproduced, exactly W0-B10's finding restated for the portal package instead of `generated/`. Not discovered earlier because no full bare root `pnpm test` had been run since `core/portal` started accumulating test files (the last full-suite baseline predates W0-J1). Fix by making the root config either delegate per-package (`pnpm -r test`, so each package's own `vitest.config.ts` governs its own files) or import/merge `core/portal/vitest.config.ts`'s settings into the root config for files under `core/portal/**` — whichever is chosen must not weaken `W0-B9`'s regen-diff gate or introduce a second, drifting copy of the alias/jsdom/jsx settings. `done:` is: `pnpm test` from the repo root, with no `-C` and no filter, actually collects and passes every existing `core/portal/src/**/*.test.tsx` file (currently `a11y.test.tsx` ×2 and any others `W0-J1`–`W0-J5` added), and `forge ci`'s test stage covers them without a new stage.
  - note: routed sonnet — this is a config-merge fix, not a design decision with the blast radius `W0-B10` has (that one touches the regen-diff invariant and a committed-artefact question; this one does not — `core/portal` is already a normal hand-maintained package with its own committed `vitest.config.ts`).

## Track C — State layer, SQLite (6 tasks)

> **Read `02_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` §10 before starting this track.** It is the dated correction that replaced Postgres with SQLite for Wave 0 and it lists eight things Phase 2 assumed that SQLite cannot do. Two of them change what may honestly be claimed.

- [x] **W0-C1** — Drizzle dual-dialect schema and the repository interface
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-A1
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#10.2, 02#10.4, 02#1.5
  - touches: core/gateway/store/**
  - done: one schema definition emits migration sets for **both** SQLite and Postgres via `drizzle-kit`; every store operation is reachable only through the repository interface; no application file outside `core/gateway/store/` imports a driver (proved by the `W0-A2` lint rule); `runtime.store.kind` is exposed on the gateway API for the portal's data-class chip.

- [x] **W0-C2** — `audit_call` and satellites, the hash chain, append-only triggers, and the result-key side table
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-C1
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#4.6, 02#10.4 (items 1, 2, 3)
  - touches: core/gateway/store/audit/**
  - done: every column in 02 §4.6 exists; `prev_hash`/`row_hash` chain correctly across inserts; `BEFORE UPDATE` and `BEFORE DELETE` triggers `RAISE(ABORT)` and a test proves both; `result_keys` is **also** normalised into `audit_result_key(call_id, key_name, key_value)` in the same transaction, indexed on `(key_value, key_name)`, and the business-key query answers in one hop; `caller_roles` is a JSON column plus an `audit_call_role` side table. **The commit message and the code comment both state that on SQLite this makes tampering detectable, not preventable.** — [P5] **and, before the first audit row exists**, the `who` block also carries `consumer_id`, `consumer_record_sha`, `consumer_auth_method`, `consumer_session_id` and `human_in_the_loop`, plus a normalised `audit_credential_ref(call_id, secret_ref, version)` satellite indexed on `secret_ref`. A test proves "which calls used this credential version" answers in one hop. **This may not be deferred to a migration** — 04 §7.5.

- [x] **W0-C3** — Idempotency records, single-use confirm nonces, and the approval-queue tables
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-C1
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.1, 02#3.1.2, 02#10.4 (item 6)
  - touches: core/gateway/store/runtime/**
  - done: a nonce is consumed atomically by a `UNIQUE`-constrained insert inside the same transaction as the execute, and a concurrent second attempt loses deterministically; the idempotency record is written **before** the binding is invoked and completed after; a replay within `scopeHours` returns the original result. A comment records that single-use holds on one node and that multi-replica is a Postgres-era property.

- [x] **W0-C4** — `forge audit verify` and the retention job
  - model: sonnet
  - deps: W0-C2
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#4.6, 02#10.4 (item 1)
  - touches: core/cli/src/commands/audit.ts
  - done: `forge audit verify --json` walks the chain and reports either intact-with-origin or the **first** broken row by id; a test that mutates a row (with triggers dropped, simulating direct file access) makes it fail at exactly that row; the retention job is the single audited exception to append-only and logs its own deletion.

- [x] **W0-C5** — Postgres-dialect parity: the same store contract suite in a second CI matrix entry
  - model: sonnet
  - deps: W0-C3
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#10.4 (item 8), 02#4.4
  - touches: tools/ci/**, core/gateway/store/**tests
  - done: the identical store contract suite passes against SQLite (temp file, default, no Docker) **and** against Postgres via Testcontainers as a second matrix entry; a dialect-specific behaviour difference fails the Postgres run rather than being papered over. This is the same discipline the dual identity providers get in `W0-D3`.

- [x] **W0-C6** — The "delete the runtime store and everything still works" test
  - model: sonnet
  - deps: W0-C1, W0-B9
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#10.3
  - touches: tools/ci/**
  - done: a CI test deletes `.mcpforge/` entirely, restarts the gateway, and asserts the full catalogue, all roles, all packages and all approval records are intact and the system is serviceable — proving no definitional data leaked into the runtime store. Audit history is expected to be empty and that is the point.

## Track F — Write safety (7 tasks, all Opus)

> **This entire track precedes track I.** Phase 1 §10.5 item 7: sequence the write-safety machinery *before* tool volume. Wave 0 is 6 write / 5 read, and this track is why that is possible.

- [x] **W0-F1** — Two-phase confirm: the `confirm` input field and the plan/execute state machine
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-E3, W0-C3
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.1, 03#7.1
  - touches: core/gateway/policy/confirm/**
  - done: every write tool's generated schema carries the nullable `confirm` field with its documented description; `confirm` absent/null returns `status: confirm_required` with `plan`, `effects`, `warnings`, `confirmToken`, `expiresAt`, `reversal` and `next` **and makes no change**, proved by asserting the mock target received no mutating call; `confirm` present executes. **It uses ordinary tool arguments only — no elicitation, no sampling, no second tool, no out-of-band channel.**

- [x] **W0-F2** — Canonical argument serialisation, `argsCanonicalHash`, and the HMAC token binding
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-F1
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.1
  - touches: core/gateway/policy/confirm/hash.ts
  - done: the canonicaliser sorts keys, normalises numbers (`1.10` ≡ `1.1`, `-0` ≡ `0`), excludes `confirm`, and is proved stable by a property test over shuffled-key and re-serialised inputs; the token payload binds `{callerSubject, toolId, toolVersion, argsCanonicalHash, planHash, nonce, exp}`; **a test plans a 100.00 amount, executes with 100000.00, and is refused with `PLAN_ARGUMENT_MISMATCH` naming the `amount` field**; tokens are single-use, TTL-bounded (300 s default) and invalid across callers and tool versions, each proved separately.

- [x] **W0-F3** — Idempotency and the visible replay
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-F2, W0-C3
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.2
  - touches: core/gateway/policy/idempotency/**
  - done: `idempotencyKey = sha256(callerSubject | toolId | toolVersion | argsCanonicalHash | confirmToken)`; the record is written before the binding is invoked; a repeat within `scopeHours` returns the **original** result with `"replayed": true` and the mock target confirms it was called exactly once. **Wave 0 exit criterion 7(d).**

- [x] **W0-F4** — The guardrail engine, including `sodConflict`, evaluated at plan *and* at execute
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-F1, W0-B8
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.3, 02#4.3

  - done: all five kinds implemented — `maxNumeric`/`minNumeric`, `allowedValues`, `rateLimit`, `sodConflict`, `timeWindow`; a test proves a guardrail that passes at plan and would breach at execute **is caught at execute**; `sodConflict` is evaluated against the caller's resolved role scope, not just the current call, and names the conflicting grant and the roles that produced it; a breach returns `POLICY_GUARDRAIL_BREACH` with the guardrail's own `message` and a `next`. **Wave 0 exit criterion 7(c).**

- [x] **W0-F5** — The reversal registry and `forge audit reverse`
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-F3, W0-C2
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.4, 03#7.5
  - touches: core/gateway/reversal/**, core/cli/src/commands/audit.ts
  - done: codegen refuses a write tool with no `reversal.class`; all four classes are represented in the type model and `irreversible` structurally forces `humanApprovalRequired: true` and `reviewPath: standard`; execute writes `reversal_class`, the reversing tool id and the extracted `result_keys` into the audit row; **`forge audit reverse <callId>` constructs the reversing call by applying `argMap` to the original result keys, and the reversal itself runs the full plan → confirm sequence**; both calls end up linked in both directions (`reverses_call_id` / `reversed_by_call_id`). **Wave 0 exit criterion 7(a).**

The suite emits a markdown evidence file for the Wave 0 checkpoint.

  - model: opus

  - wave: 0

  - touches: tests/write-path/**, core/gateway/policy/guardrails/**, core/codegen/schema/**, core/shared/src/manifest/**
  - done: **flagged 8 Sep 2026, fixing an incident, not a fresh feature.** `tests/write-path/support/store-world.ts`'s criterion-(a) test builds its catalogue entry from `policy.fixtures.ts`, which (1) stubs `guardrailEvaluator` to unconditionally return `{breached:false}` — the real evaluator is never wired into this test at all — and (2) hand-writes `WriteSafetyView` objects for `voucherCreate`/`voucherCancel` that **disagree with the shipped manifests** (fixture: `humanApprovalRequired:false`, `reversal.class:'native-reverse'`; manifest: `humanApprovalRequired:true`, `reversal.class:irreversible`). Together these meant the criterion-(a) test could pass — and did pass — even while `voucher.cancel` was completely unreachable in the real system (its now-removed bogus `requiresField` guardrail refused every call at stage 6f, before the plan/confirm/execute path F7(a) claims to prove was ever reached). Fix: (1) drive the criterion-(a) test's catalogue entry from the real compiled manifest artefacts (the same `generated/tools/**` output the gateway actually serves) and the real `guardrailEvaluator`, not hand-written fixtures — if that requires wiring the still-not-committed generated-test harness from `W0-B10`, coordinate with that task rather than duplicating a second ad-hoc resolver; (2) regenerate `EVIDENCE.md`'s criterion (a) entry and confirm by inspection that it now names the real manifest's `reversal.class`/`humanApprovalRequired`, not the fixture's; (3) retire `requiresField` from both `core/codegen/schema/tool.schema.json`'s guardrail-kind enum and `core/shared/src/manifest/**`'s `GuardrailKind` type (it is not one of 02 §3.1.3's five real kinds, `W0-F4`'s own `done:` criterion lists exactly five, and leaving it schema-legal means any future manifest can reintroduce this identical outage with `forge validate` passing it clean) — update `core/gateway/policy/guardrails/gate.ts` and `guardrails.test.ts:393` accordingly, since that test currently asserts the wrong-but-tolerated behaviour as correct; (4) `forge validate`/`forge codegen` stay clean and byte-identical throughout. Do not weaken `voucher.cancel`'s actual write-safety posture (`humanApprovalRequired`, `reversal.class: irreversible`, `idempotency.scopeHours: 24`) while doing this — the fix is to the *test*, the *schema*, and the *evaluator agreement*, not the control.
  - **related, same incident, do here or split out explicitly if scope grows:** `generated/tools/**/handler.generated.ts`'s local `evaluateGuardrails` only implements `maxNumeric`/`allowedValues` and silently ignores every other declared kind (`sodConflict`, `rateLimit`, `timeWindow`, and would have silently passed a `requiresField` the real gateway evaluator fails closed on) — a live fail-open divergence between the generated handler and the gateway's own evaluator. Currently moot for `voucher.cancel` (its guardrail list is now empty) but not moot in general. Codegen's guardrail-emission template (`W0-B6`'s output) should either implement all five real kinds or explicitly delegate to the one shared `guardrailEvaluator` rather than reimplementing a subset.

The failures are TWO further genuine, distinct defects, not the one this task was scoped to fix, each with real blast radius outside `next.config.ts`:

Separately, `/build` and `/build/draft-1`'s `yaml-editor.tsx`/`draft-editor.tsx` pull in `core/codegen/src/schema/index.ts`, which uses `node:fs`, into the client bundle the same way (`UnhandledSchemeError: node:fs`). `serverExternalPackages` does not help either case — it only excludes a package from the *server* bundle. The real fix is a narrower, client-safe subpath export off `@mcpforge/gateway/store` and `@mcpforge/codegen/schema` (or moving these pages' data loading server-side) — a portal/gateway/codegen **source** change, not a bundler-config change, and it touches the `core/gateway/store` barrel that Opus-guarded modules (audit, nonce, idempotency) sit beside. Outside this task's authorized widened `touches`.
    - **(b) real WCAG color-contrast failures, light theme only (`/catalog`, `/build`):** axe's `color-contrast` rule (serious, WCAG 2 AA 1.4.3) fails on the `status-write` badge (`#9a6110` on `#ede9e5`, 4.24:1, needs 4.5:1) and the `status-neutral` badge (`#6e7379` on `#f4f5f7`, 4.38:1, needs 4.5:1) — both `bold`/`10.5px` chip text using tokens from `tokens.primitives.css`. This is a genuine design-token contrast defect, live in production today, not a bundler artefact — but fixing it means darkening shared status-chip tokens used across the whole design system (`no-raw-color` territory), which is exactly the "real design-system accessibility violation" CLAUDE.md §8 says to flag rather than paper over with a local, one-off patch. Flagged for whoever owns `core/portal/src/design/tokens.primitives.css`.

  - **Named follow-up landed (this entry) — the App Router Link-navigation defect (follow-up (1) above) is FIXED; gate 4 is now 4 of 6.** Root cause, actually observed (not guessed): `PlanExpiryCountdown` (`core/portal/src/components/write-path/plan-expiry-countdown.tsx`) seeds its `remaining` state via `useState(compute)`, where `compute()` reads `Date.now()` (or the injected clock) at render time — so the SSR pass and the client hydration pass, seconds apart in dev mode, compute different values for the same `data-remaining` attribute and `expires in …` text. React 19 treats that as a hydration mismatch (confirmed via a `pageerror` listener: "Hydration failed... this tree will be regenerated on the client", firing on every `/approvals` load) and discards+remounts the whole affected subtree client-side. That remount was observed to intermittently race an in-flight `Link` click's RSC navigation — the fetch completes (200, visible on the network) but the URL never updates — reproducing `approve.spec.ts`'s exact symptom on 3 of 5 unmodified reruns. **Fix:** `suppressHydrationWarning` on the two DOM nodes whose content is legitimately clock-derived (the `data-remaining` div and the `plan-expiry-text` span) — the documented React fix for a "current time" node — so the mismatch is silenced at the node instead of triggering a subtree-wide discard/remount. No router/navigation code touched. **Verified:** `approve.spec.ts` passed cleanly on 5/5 solo reruns post-fix (was intermittently failing pre-fix, confirmed by 3 solo reruns without the fix: 2 pass, 1 fail); a `pageerror` probe confirmed zero hydration errors on `/approvals` post-fix; `/catalog/[toolId]`'s "Back to Catalog" link (the prior agent's cited unrelated-link repro) navigates correctly by both keyboard Enter and a real mouse `.click()`. Full `tests/a11y/keyboard/*` run (2 workers): **4 passed** (plan-confirm-execute, reverse, facet-filtering, **approve** — newly passing) **2 failed** (role-edit, palette-search — unchanged, separate named follow-ups (2) and (3) above, not touched). `facet-filtering` flickered failed once under 4-worker fan-out but passed solo and under 2 workers — the pre-documented dev-server compile-queue flake (playwright.config.ts's own comment), not a regression. `tools/ci/src/stages.ts` stage 17 (`a11yKeyboardFlows`) detail text updated to report 4/6 and name this fix; it still returns `status: 'failed'` (correctly — 2 of 6 flows remain blocked) and is still never marked allowed-to-fail. Full regression, this pass: repo-root `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` both clean. `pnpm -C core/portal test`: see this task's closing report for the exact count (no source file outside `plan-expiry-countdown.tsx` and `tools/ci/src/stages.ts` was touched). Checkbox still correctly left unticked — this closes one of three named follow-ups, not the gate.
  - **Named follow-up landed (this entry) — the missing-ChangeHost half of follow-up (2) above is FIXED; the whole flow is still blocked by a second, distinct defect, so gate 4 stays 4 of 6.** Root cause, actually observed: `useOptionalChangeHost()` really did return `undefined` on every real route — no route anywhere composed `ChangeHostProvider`. Fixed by composing a real, `LocalGit`-backed `ChangeHost` at the root layout: `components/shell/app-change-host.tsx` (`'use client'`, wraps `{children}` in `<ChangeHostProvider host={defaultChangeHost}>`, mounted in `app/layout.tsx` beside `TooltipProvider`) supplies `lib/change-host/default-host.ts`'s `defaultChangeHost`, whose eight methods are thin calls into `lib/change-host/local-git-actions.ts` — genuine `'use server'` Server Actions, not a constructed `LocalGit` instance passed down as a prop (an instance closing over `execFile`/`node:fs` state cannot cross the RSC boundary as an ordinary prop; only a Server Action reference survives it). Those actions lazily seed ONE persistent sandbox working tree per server process under `.mcpforge/change-host-sandbox/` (copying `manifests/ roles/ packages/ consumers/ enums/ evals/ approvals/ generated/` plus the root prettier/editorconfig files, mirroring `compile-role.ts`'s own `COPY_DIRS`), then construct `LocalGit` against that sandbox — never this repo's own working tree, which `change-host.contract.test.ts` explicitly forbids and which genuinely has no `.git` here regardless. The sandbox persists across the whole server process (module-scoped cache) so a `saveDraft` and a later `propose` on the same branch land as two commits on the same branch, matching `RoleEditor`'s real two-call flow. `ChangeHostError`'s `code`/`.next` do not reliably survive a thrown-Error round trip through a Server Action, so every action that can fail returns a plain `ActionResult<T>` (`{ok:true,value}` / `{ok:false,code,message,next}`) instead of throwing;   - **This entry — root-cause investigation of the four pre-existing flows' "contention," per the prior session's `local-git-actions.ts`/`ensureHost()` lead. One real, distinct concurrency bug FOUND AND FIXED; the "contention" label itself DISPROVEN as the primary cause of these four flows' flakiness.**
    - **Real bug found and fixed (in scope, not `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS`):** `ensureHost()` in `core/portal/src/lib/change-host/local-git-actions.ts` had a TOCTOU race — every route mounts `AppChangeHostProvider` (`app/layout.tsx`), whose effect calls `changeHostCurrentBranch()`/`changeHostDescribeRemote()` on mount, both routed through `ensureHost()`. Two concurrent invocations before `cachedHost` was set could both observe no `.mcpforge/change-host-sandbox/.git` and both run `seedSandbox()`'s `git init`/`config`×3/`add`/`commit` against the SAME working tree concurrently. Fixed by caching the in-flight promise itself (single-flight), not just the resolved value, so concurrent callers await the one seeding operation; a failed seed clears the slot so a later call can retry. This is real and worth keeping, but **it is not what was causing `approve`/`facet-filtering`/`plan-confirm-execute`/`reverse` to fail** — only `role-edit.spec.ts` exercises `saveDraft`/`propose`; the other three never touch `ChangeHost` at all, confirmed by reading each spec.
    - **The concurrency hypothesis itself was directly disproven, not just deprioritized:** ran the full 6-spec suite with `--workers=1` (fully serial, zero cross-spec concurrency possible) — it failed WORSE (4 of 6) than a `--workers=2` run taken minutes earlier (3 of 6), and on a different set of specs each time. If cross-spec/cross-worker resource contention (git sandbox or otherwise) were the cause, serial execution would be reliably clean; it was not.
    - **Actual root cause, confirmed from captured errors, not guessed:** every observed failure across all runs was a normal Playwright default-timeout (5000ms, unmodified per-`expect` default) on `toBeVisible()`/`toContainText()`/`toHaveAttribute()` against an element that is present once the target route's client bundle finishes compiling — i.e. genuine first-hit on-demand dev-server compile latency on a cold `next dev` (started fresh each run, per this task's own clean-state discipline) outrunning short per-assertion timeouts, exactly the class of cost `playwright.config.ts`'s own header already named as a suspect but had not pinned to a specific mechanism. Confirmed directly for `approve.spec.ts`: its failure was on the region inside the dynamic `/approvals/[approvalId]` detail route — a route no prior warm-up or navigation had ever hit before the timed assertion.
    - **Fix applied:** a new `tests/a11y/keyboard/global-setup.ts`, wired via `playwright.config.ts`'s `globalSetup`, that sequentially (not concurrently — warming must not recreate the compile-queue effect it's paying down) GETs every route the six specs navigate to — including `/approvals/warmup-probe`, a placeholder id that still forces Next.js to compile the dynamic `[approvalId]` route module — once, before any timed test runs. This is fix-class (b) from this task's brief (dev-server compile-queue, route warm-up), not (a) — the concurrency-serialization path was tried, found not to be the actual fix, and reverted (`test:a11y:keyboard` script kept at `--workers=2`, unchanged from before this entry).
    - **Effect, honestly reported — reduced but did not eliminate flakiness:** three clean, consecutive full-suite runs after the warm-up fix (each from a genuinely clean state — processes verified killed via `Get-Process node`, `.mcpforge/change-host-sandbox/` deleted): **run 1: 5/6** (approve failed — the dynamic-route case, before `/approvals/warmup-probe` was added to the warm list); **run 2 (after adding the dynamic-route warm-up): 4/6** (approve AND facet-filtering failed — facet-filtering had been clean on every prior run this session, including pre-fix); **stopped at 2 of the required 5 verification runs** because the pattern across all attempts (varying failing subsets, not a fixed 4, not eliminated by either serialization or route warm-up) indicates residual per-test execution-time variance on this machine that is not fully explained by either candidate mechanism this task was scoped to check.
    - **Per CLAUDE.md §8 / this task's own item 9: reporting this honestly rather than forcing further timeout-widening or another plausible-but-unproven mechanism.** The two fixes landed this entry are real and correct on their own terms (the `ensureHost()` race is a genuine bug independent of whether it explains this suite's flakiness; the route warm-up measurably reduced first-hit timeouts) but neither, alone or together, delivers the reliable 6/6 this task's own verification bar requires. What remains unexplained: `facet-filtering.spec.ts` failing on run 2 (`toHaveAttribute` on `:focus`, a pure client-side keyboard-focus assertion with no network/compile dependency at all) after passing clean on every earlier run this session — this looks like genuine UI-animation/focus timing sensitivity under CPU load rather than compile latency or git contention, a third, different mechanism this task did not have budget to chase down. **This needs a further, focused pass** — most likely widening default `expect` timeouts suite-wide (not per-spec ad hoc) via `playwright.config.ts`'s `expect.timeout`, now that the warm-up has removed the compile-latency component and isolated what looks like a smaller, genuine execution-speed margin problem on this specific machine — verified over enough consecutive runs to actually prove 6/6, which this entry did not reach.
    - **No regressions:** repo-root `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` clean, all 15 workspaces. `pnpm -C core/portal test`: **614/614 passed, 71/71 files** — unchanged from the recorded baseline (confirmed as the current baseline before this entry's changes, per this task's own instruction to verify rather than assume). `tools/ci/src/stages.ts` stage 17 (`a11yKeyboardFlows`) unchanged in this entry — still genuinely spawns `pnpm -C core/portal run test:a11y:keyboard` and reports the real exit code, never `not_implemented`, never allowed-to-fail.
    - **Files touched, all within `touches`:** `core/portal/src/lib/change-host/local-git-actions.ts` (the `ensureHost()` single-flight fix); `core/portal/tests/a11y/keyboard/global-setup.ts` (new); `core/portal/playwright.config.ts` (`globalSetup` wiring only, `workers`/`timeout` unchanged). `core/portal/package.json`'s `test:a11y:keyboard` script was tried at `--workers=1` during investigation and reverted to `--workers=2` — no net change from before this entry. Nothing under `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS` touched or needed.

Gates 3 and 5 and the graph/table-toggle disposition are unchanged from the immediately preceding entry (re-confirmed, not re-derived). Verified: `pnpm -C core/portal typecheck` and `lint` both clean; `pnpm -C core/portal test` 614/614 (confirmed as the pre-existing baseline first, then unchanged after every edit); `pnpm -C core/portal run test:a11y:routes` 34/34 (an intermediate run showed transient timeouts/`ECONNRESET` under the dev server's load plus one near-miss `color-contrast` failure on `/requests` dark theme unrelated to this task's `touches` — a rerun with nothing else changed came back 34/34 clean, confirming those were load-induced flakes, not regressions); `pnpm exec playwright test tests/a11y/keyboard/palette-search.spec.ts` passes solo against the now-global palette; `pnpm -C core/portal build` prerenders all 20 routes with zero errors.

## Track P — Completeness gaps found by the 24 Sep 2026 source audit (19 tasks)

Added 24 Sep 2026 after a full-source audit the user asked for ("any orphan API end points not being called, any specific functionality missing, anything not as per initial goal"). **None of these is an exit-criterion failure** — every Wave 0 criterion in the table below still has a passing task. They are gaps the task list never gated, because the J-track `done:` clauses were component-level ("renders X, passes axe") and so "built and tested" never implied "mounted and connected to a live source". `W0-J22` (`AppShell` built but mounted nowhere) was the first instance of this bug class; `W0-P2`/`W0-P3`/`W0-P6` are the same class again. Read this preamble before picking up any task in this track — the shared root cause matters more than the individual symptoms.

- [x] **W0-P1** — Wire CI stages 2, 4 and 11 to their existing implementations
  - model: opus
  - deps: none
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#7.2, 04#3
  - touches: tools/ci/**
  - context: `tools/ci/src/stages.ts` hardcodes three stages to `notImplemented(...)` — stage 2 `forge validate` (the ~40 schema + policy rules, `notImplemented('W0-B2')`), stage 4 the custom-binding contract-hash drift check (`notImplemented('W0-B5')`), stage 11 `slice-diff-proof` (`notImplemented('a later Track M/package task')`). **All three implementations already exist and work**: `core/cli/src/commands/validate.ts` (passes 17/17 manifests today), `core/codegen/src/emit/hash.ts` (already called for real by the portal's own `build/_lib/checks.ts`), and `core/cli/src/commands/slice-diff.ts`. `grep -rn "runValidate|sliceDiff" tools/ci/src` returns nothing — they are simply not connected. Because `ok` is true when only `passed`/`not_implemented` stages exist (`tools/ci/src/index.ts`), **`forge ci` reports green today without ever validating a manifest, checking contract drift, or proving the no-fork claim.** That is not literally "allowed to fail" (the file's own header is careful about the distinction) but the effect on a green pipeline is the same, and CLAUDE.md §5 forbids the neighbour.
  - done: `pnpm -C tools/ci test` passes and stages 2, 4 and 11 each return `passed` or `failed` against the **real repo** — never `not_implemented`; `notImplemented(...)` has no remaining call site for stages 2/4/11; each stage runs the real implementation as a subprocess (the `bench-gate.ts` precedent and its header's reasoning for why it is a subprocess and not an import applies unchanged); each fails closed on a missing prerequisite rather than soft-passing; `stages.test.ts`'s `not_implemented` fixture list drops to `[]` (it is `[2, 4, 11]` today) with a test proving each of the three genuinely spawns its command and names the failing stage on breach; `forge ci` run end-to-end locally reports the three by name in its summary.
  - **Status: done, with one gate now legitimately red — read the next paragraph before "fixing" it.** Three new modules, one per gate, each following `bench-gate.ts`'s established one-module-per-stage shape: `tools/ci/src/validate-gate.ts` (stage 2), `tools/ci/src/contract-check.ts` (stage 4), `tools/ci/src/slice-diff-gate.ts` (stage 11). `notImplemented(...)` is **deleted** — it had no call sites left and `noUnusedLocals` would have failed the build; `StageStatus` keeps `'not_implemented'` in its union deliberately (it is the right report for a future stage whose capability really does not exist) and `stages.ts`'s header now records that no stage carries it as of this task.
  - **Observed against the real repo** (each gate run directly; output recorded here because this is the evidence the task turns on): **stage 2 PASSED** — "17 manifest file(s) checked, 0 failures", *plus two real warnings that CI has never printed before*: `sod.declared-conflict` and `sod.implicit-create-approve`, both on `roles/p2p.yaml`'s create/approve pair on `jde.scm.purchase_order`, disposition `warn-and-require-exception`. They are warnings, not failures (W0-B8), but they name a recorded exception that 02 §4.3 requires and `approvals/` should be checked for. **Stage 4 PASSED** — 4 hand-owned binding bodies checked (`jde.ap.voucher.cancel`/`.create`, `jde.fin.journal.create`/`.submit`), every contract-hash matches. **Stage 11 FAILED** — and correctly: the repo defines exactly one slice (`packages/jde-fin.yaml`) and a two-slice proof cannot run against one slice.
  - **The stage-11 decision, recorded because a later reader will be tempted to undo it.** Three options existed and they are not equivalent: (a) leave it `not_implemented` — hides an unproven claim behind a green pipeline, which is the thing this task exists to stop; (b) diff `jde-fin` against itself — trivially identical, a manufactured pass, forbidden; (c) fail, naming the missing artefact. The gate does (c). **A second slice definition is a human-authored artefact** — a real selection for a real deployment — and CLAUDE.md §8 forbids an agent inventing one, so this is a `needs_human`: either author `packages/<id>.yaml`, or record the written waiver this file's own exit-criteria rule already demands ("a fail blocks PROCEED without a written waiver naming who accepted the risk and until when"). **Do not soften the gate to make CI green.**
  - **Tests:** `validate-gate.test.ts` (6), `contract-check.test.ts` (9 — the pass case runs against the REAL repo, because "every hand-owned body in this repository matches its manifest" is not a claim a fixture can make; each failure mode is then proven by copying the real `manifests/`+`generated/` trees into a throwaway root and perturbing exactly one thing), `slice-diff-gate.test.ts` (10 — including an explicit test that the one-slice case fails *and that no runner was invoked*, i.e. no self-comparison was attempted). `stages.test.ts`'s `not_implemented` fixture list is now `[]` (was `[2, 4, 11]`) with per-stage assertions on the fixture behaviour of each.

W0-K2's own status note records this check finding **zero** violations when it landed, so the violations arrived with the consumer-governance portal work after it (N-track / the 15 Sep W0-HG8 session). The fix is an R8 security-boundary judgement — either the portal stops importing those subpaths in-process, or each becomes a deliberate, commented allowlist entry — and it is deliberately **not** made here: it is outside this task's `touches: tools/ci/**` in spirit if not in letter, and CLAUDE.md §1 forbids tidying adjacent code mid-task. - [x] **W0-P2** — The portal↔gateway read seam — design note first
  - model: opus
  - deps: none
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#6.5, 02#7.1, 03#3, 03#13
  - touches: docs/build-plan/w0-p2-portal-gateway-seam.md
  - context: **The root gap of this track.** The portal has zero API routes (`find core/portal/src/app -name route.ts` returns 0 files) and zero `fetch` calls to the gateway — the only three mentions of `fetch` in the portal are comments saying a later task will wire it (`components/palette/find-client.ts:23`, `command-palette.tsx:14`). The gateway exposes exactly `/mcp`, the two metadata paths and the deliberate DCR 403 (`core/gateway/transport/http.ts`) — correct per "no REST facade", but it means **there is no route by which the portal could read runtime state even if it tried.** Consequence: 13 of 22 portal pages read hand-written fixtures (Home, Approvals, Approval detail, Activity, Activity→Consumers, Call detail, Insights, Requests, Build, all three Environments tabs), and the fixtures are layered — `home/fixtures.ts` imports `approvals/fixtures`, `activity/fixtures`, `catalog/fixtures`, `requests/fixtures`, so Home is a fixture composed of fixtures. The definitional half of the portal **is** real (Governance reads `roles/`/`consumers/` and compiles them; Build's checks pane runs a real `forge validate` in a sandbox; propose/discard is a real `LocalGit`) — it is specifically the **runtime** half (audit, calls, approvals, integrity chain, consumer usage, probe status) that has no source.
  - done: a written design note, reviewed by the owner before any code, deciding and justifying: (a) which gateway read surface the portal consumes and **how that surface exists at all** given `/mcp` is the only door — whether the portal calls the four meta-tools over MCP as a registered consumer (`portal-local` is already registered for exactly this, 05 §1.3.3), or a separate read API is added, or Next server components read the store directly — **naming the R8 HTTP-only boundary consequence of the choice either way**, because `tools/ci/src/portal-http-boundary.ts` will police it and the allowlist it maintains is the record of that decision; (b) how each existing `*Source` interface (`HomeSource` and its siblings — they were built as seams for precisely this swap) is satisfied by the live client; (c) what a page shows when the gateway is down, which is the normal state on a laptop, and how that differs visibly from "no data"; (d) whether fixtures survive as the test/Storybook source or are deleted. **This task produces the note and nothing else** — it is a `needs_human` review point per CLAUDE.md §8, since the seam is a security boundary and a file-format decision at once.
  - **Status: note written 24 Sep 2026, AWAITING THE OWNER'S DECISION — left unticked deliberately, because the `done:` criterion says "reviewed by the owner before any code" and that review has not happened.** The note is `docs/build-plan/w0-p2-portal-gateway-seam.md`. No code was written.
  - **The finding that changes the framing:** R8 is not being violated by a shortcut — **it is unimplementable as written.** 02 §6.5 and 03 §1 both say the portal "talks to the gateway only over its HTTP API", but that API was never specified and does not exist: the gateway serves `/mcp`, two metadata paths and a deliberate 403. So the gap is a missing interface, not a sloppy portal.
  - **Three options, one recommendation.** (A) portal becomes an MCP client — **rejected**: MCP has no vocabulary for audit rows, approval queues, integrity chains or consumer usage, so it would mean minting `forge.audit.*`-style tools that put governance data in the **agent-visible** catalogue and spend the ≤1,300-token role budget on tools no agent should call. (B) Next server components read `./.mcpforge/runtime.db` directly — **rejected**: exactly what R8 forbids, breaks under `MCPFORGE_MODE=headless` / Mode B / Postgres-multi-replica, and routes around the policy chain. (C) ★ **a read-only, consumer-authenticated, policy-filtered governance HTTP API on the gateway** (`/api/v1/**`, reads only — writes stay on `/mcp` and git). Keeps R8 honest, keeps governance data out of the tool catalogue, and extends non-negotiable #6's consumer ∩ human intersection to portal reads, which is the precondition for `W0-P4` to mean anything.
  - **The test of whether `W0-P3` is done right:** `tools/ci/src/portal-http-boundary.ts`'s allowlist should get **smaller**, never larger — under Option C the portal imports nothing from `@mcpforge/gateway` at runtime.
  - **The owner's actual decision is narrower than it looks:** does "no REST facade" (CLAUDE.md §3) govern only agent-facing *discovery*, as every occurrence of it in 02 §5 reads to me, or is it absolute? If absolute, Option C is out and the runtime half of the portal cannot be made real without accepting (A)'s catalogue corruption or (B)'s fictional headless mode. **That trade should be made knowingly, not discovered mid-`W0-P3`.**
  - **DECIDED 25 Sep 2026 — Option C, owner-accepted. Ticked.** Recorded in the note's §7. The owner accepted the recommendation (*"I am unclear what's best as per my expectation, we can go with it"*) rather than choosing independently, and §7 says so for any later reviewer. **What is now settled:** "no REST facade" governs agent-facing discovery and invocation only; `/api/v1/**` is **runtime reads only** (definitional reads stay on git); no write endpoint is authorized. `W0-P3` is unblocked, and `W0-P7` should land before or with it.

    1. *"Split into P3a–P3d (Recommended)"*.

It is `GET /api/v1/calls` (paged; filters tool, consumer and outcome) · `/calls/{callId}` · `/audit/verify` · `/approvals?status=pending|decided` · `/approvals/{approvalId}` · `/consumers/usage?window=24h|7d` · `/enablement` · `/deployment`.     3. **Row filter:** *"Same scope as tools/list (Recommended)"*. A row is visible when its tool is in the viewer's resolved scope (the consumer's authorizations intersected with the human's roles), or when the viewer made it. Integrity verification is visible to every signed-in viewer, as counts and never row contents. **Implementation note, recorded because it narrows the wording:** "same scope as tools/list" is built as `resolveReadAuthority` = **Deployed ∩ Granted ∩ ConsumerAuthorized**. That is the wording of the option as asked ("authorizations intersected with the human's roles"), not the full six-way `visible()`. The other three predicates describe what *works now*, not who *may see*. Activated is a per-MCP-session lens that a stateless read does not have. ProbeEnabled and ¬KillSwitched would hide a killed tool's history from the people who answer for it, and with no probe report (every fresh checkout) they would hide **every** row. If the owner meant the full six, that is a one-line change to `READ_AUTHORITY_PREDICATES`.

  - **Status: done 27 Sep 2026, branch `forge/W0-P3a-gateway-read-api`.** `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS` touched: `core/gateway/scope/**` (`resolve.ts`: new `READ_AUTHORITY_PREDICATES` + `resolveReadAuthority`; `resolveScope`'s behaviour is unchanged, and the shared loop is private) and `core/gateway/store/audit*` (a READ method only).     - **Gateway:** `core/gateway/api/v1/read-api.ts` `createReadApi`, routed from `transport/http.ts` (`readApi` option; absent, the paths are a plain 404) and composed in `launch.ts` with the SAME `consumerAuth` object `/mcp` uses. Per request: `[2a]` authenticates, then `sessions.establish` (it is pure: no row written and no session slot held), then `resolveReadAuthority` over `catalogue.entries`. The method is checked first (405, `Allow: GET`), then authentication, then routing, so an unauthenticated caller cannot tell which paths exist. A row that exists but is not readable returns the same 404 as a missing one. Refusals use `{error:{code,message,next,correlationId}}`, with statuses matching `/mcp`'s front door.
    - **Store (both dialects):** `audit.listRecent({visibleToolIds, ownSubject, toolId?, consumerId?, outcome?, beforeId?, limit})`, newest first, with the authority inside the SQL (`tool_id in (…) or caller_subject = ?`). An empty authority reads nothing, and the limit is capped at 500. `approvals.listDecided({visibleToolIds, ownSubject, limit})`. The pending queue is filtered after `listPending`, because it is small and expiring by construction. Usage is read bucket-by-bucket with the existing `getBucket`, so it needed no store change. The audit append-only guard test lists `listRecent` as a READ method.
    - **Contract:** `core/shared/src/api/v1/index.ts` (zod is now a `@mcpforge/shared` dependency, same `^3.24.1`). Call detail is the full `AuditCallRecord`, plus the approval it went through. That approval is found by plan hash, and there is no index for that yet, so the lookup scans pending plus decided for the tool; a miss is `null`. `/deployment` omits the store's filesystem location. `/enablement` returns `status: null` when no probe report names a tool, never an invented status.

It covers: no consumer, human-only, consumer-only, a replayed assertion, POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE, path hiding, in-scope vs out-of-scope vs own rows, a human with no roles, the **same human through a narrower consumer**, 404 parity, the paging cursor, approval queue and id, enablement, and the aggregates, all parsed with the shared schemas. `scope/read-authority.test.ts` passes 8/8: no probe report and a kill switch do not hide history, activation is ignored, and each of the three predicates is still load-bearing. The store contract passes on SQLite, and on **Postgres 22/22** via `pnpm test:postgres`, the first run of that leg now that Docker is available. `errors/enumeration.test.ts` passes 147/147, with two new computed-`next` rules, each driven through real code. Gateway: 1,265 passed plus the new tests (the one failure was the enumeration rule, now fixed). `pnpm test:policy` is 121/122 (the known W0-P12 tripwire). `forge validate` exits 0 and the regen-diff is clean.

They render as their own chips (`catalog-status-chip.tsx`) and facet options, and a failed read shows ONE `LiveStateNotice` above a catalogue that still renders from git. Consumption: `loadToolConsumption` counts one tool's last 30 days from `/calls?tool=`, on the tool page and drawer only, labelled "calls you may read", capped at 5 pages of 200 and then shown as "at least N"; an unreadable result shows its message and `next`, never zero.

- [x] **W0-P25** — A human decision path for runtime approvals (OWNER DECISION FIRST)
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-P3a
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 02#3.1.1, w0-p2-portal-gateway-seam.md §7, CLAUDE.md §2 items 4 and 6
  - touches: (decided by the owner's choice) core/gateway/policy/approval/**, core/gateway/transport/**, core/portal/src/app/approvals/**
  - context: Found in W0-P3b, 28 Sep 2026. The approval gate can decide (`policy/approval/gate.ts` `decide()`: self-approval refused, expiry, SQL-guarded transition), but nothing exposes it. `/mcp` serves tools, and `/api/v1` is read-only by decision. With no probe evidence every Wave 0 write needs per-call approval, so writes cannot complete end to end. Options to put to the owner: (a) a narrow, separately-prefixed decision endpoint (e.g. `POST /api/v1/approvals/{id}/decision`, which W0-P2 §7 says needs a fresh decision) behind the same consumer ∩ human front door, with the approver's own grants checked; (b) an approver-only MCP tool; (c) a `forge approve` CLI (no authenticated session today, like `forge kill`). Recommendation to bring: (a), because it keeps approvals out of the agent catalogue and uses the human's own session.
  - done: the owner has chosen; the chosen path decides through `gate.decide()` only (never the repository directly), refuses self-approval and a non-approver, writes its audit evidence, and carries a `next` on every refusal; the portal's approval page decides through it; `pnpm test:policy` covers the refusals.
  - **DECIDED 30 Sep 2026 — option (a).** The owner: "go with option (a) for P25". Build `POST /api/v1/approvals/{id}/decision`, separately prefixed from the read routes, behind the same consumer ∩ human front door, with the approver's own grants checked. Not yet implemented: this task is `model: opus` and touches `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS`, so it waits for an Opus session.
  - **Two more owner decisions, 30 Sep 2026, at the start of the build (the spec was silent on both; asked with recommendations, both chosen as "(Recommended)"):** (1) **Who is an approver:** anyone other than the requester whose own authority includes the tool. Authority is `resolveReadAuthority`, which is Deployed ∩ Granted ∩ ConsumerAuthorized (the human's roles ∩ the portal consumer's authorizations), and the consumer must allow writes. No role-schema change. A known consequence: the local groups map to roles that do not exist, so no local user can approve anything until that mapping is fixed. (2) **Audit evidence:** a new `approve` audit phase. Every decision (approved or rejected) and every refused attempt writes a hash-chained audit row, as well as updating the `approval_request` row.
  - **DONE 30 Sep 2026.** Gateway: `core/gateway/api/v1/approval-decision.ts` serves `POST /api/v1/approvals/{id}/decision` behind the read API's front door (`read-api.ts` routes that one path; every other non-GET is still 405). It refuses a consumer without `writeAllowed` (`CONSUMER_NOT_AUTHORIZED`), then refuses an approval outside the actor's read authority with a 404 identical to a missing one, then calls `gate.decide()` with the approver taken from the token. The decision and its `approve` audit row commit in one `store.transaction`; a refused attempt commits its row alone. The confirm token is never returned: only its hash goes into the row, which links the decision to the execute that spends it. `launch.ts` now builds ONE `approvalGate` shared by stage 6g and the API. `refusal.ts` holds the shared `ApiRefusal` and status table. Contract: `approvalDecisionRequestSchema` (strict, so it has no approver field; a decline needs a reason), `approvalDecisionResponseSchema`, and `approve` added to `AUDIT_PHASES`, `CALL_PHASES`, `AuditPhase` and the usage input's phase. No migration is needed because `phase` is free text. Portal: `app/approvals/[approvalId]/actions.ts` (server action) and `decide-form.tsx` on the approval page, with `decideApproval()` added to the one gateway client. Tests: `tests/policy/escalation.approval-decision.test.ts` 11/11 against the real launched gateway; `pnpm test:policy` 132/133 (the 1 is the known P12 tripwire); gateway 1268 pass (the 2 driver-isolation timeouts pass alone, the known load flake); portal 833/833 plus the new form and client tests; lint and typecheck clean.
  - **Choices made in the build (the spec was silent, small blast radius):** a refused attempt whose approval the actor may not read names the pseudo tool id `forge.approval.decide` in its audit row, because the row is the actor's own and they could read the real tool id back from it. Every `approve` row carries `resultKeys: [{approvalId}]`, so every attempt on one request is one indexed lookup. `isWrite: false`, so an `approve` row counts as one call in the consumer's usage and as nothing else. Malformed bodies are refused with `INPUT_INVALID` and not audited, because nothing was attempted.
  - **Open for the owner (not built, flagged):** (1) Any registered consumer with `writeAllowed` can reach this endpoint, including an agent holding a human's token, so an agent could approve as its human. Limiting decisions to human-operated consumers would need a new consumer-record field. (2) The endpoint is not under the caps rate limiter. (3) Like `plan` and `reject` rows today, an `approve` row also writes a consumption edge (portal consumer → tool). (4) No live browser walkthrough was recorded, and it cannot show a success locally until the groups-to-roles mapping names real roles. (5) The fixture-backed `approval-decision.tsx` / `ApproverDecisionPanel` stays unmounted; that is W0-P3d.

The persona pill and account button are static props, so everyone who opens the portal is implicitly a full admin across every governance screen, every approval and every kill switch. 03 §2 specified otherwise and was explicit: persona switching *"is bound to the caller's actual roles: a person only sees personas they hold. A developer who is not an approver sees no approver persona"* — with the equally explicit constraint that persona **never hides a page**, because *"hiding governance surfaces from the people being governed is the failure mode this product exists to prevent."* So the target is a lens plus **action** gating, not page-level RBAC. `W0-J6`'s `done:` clause only required the topbar to *carry* a persona pill, which it does, as a placeholder — the gate passed and the absent backend was never assigned to anyone. The gateway side is sound: non-negotiable #6's consumer ∩ human intersection is really enforced for agent calls. This is portal-only. Live consequence today: nothing stops the same person who proposes a change from approving it, which is invisible with one user and is a real control failure with two.
  - done: a written design note, reviewed by the owner before any code, deciding: (a) which `IdentityProvider` the portal resolves its viewer through (the seam exists and is contract-tested for both the local store and OIDC — this is a wiring choice, not a new mechanism); (b) which **actions** are gated — at minimum Propose, Approve, Kill, `issue-credential` — and the refusal copy for each, which must satisfy non-negotiable #5's agent-actionable `next` in its human form; (c) **whether proposer is required to differ from approver**, and if so where it is enforced, since that is the concrete control the current build lacks; (d) what "full access for admin" means precisely, enumerated, given 03 §2 forbids hiding pages from anyone; (e) how the persona pill's tooltip states that the gateway enforces grants regardless of the selected pill, per 03 §2. **Note and nothing else** — `core/portal/src/components/write-path/**` and any approval gating are `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS`, and "who may approve" is a security boundary the spec did not fully settle.
  - **DECIDED 26 Sep 2026 — ticked.** All six decisions are in the note's §9: multi-IdP sign-in (local / Entra / OCI IAM / Agentis, several at once, issuer-qualified subjects); personas from the git mapping; proposer ≠ approver except an **admin** may self-approve a definitional change (flagged `selfApproved`, a validate warning; runtime writes keep the gateway's SoD); Kill shows `forge kill`; `portal-local` → `private-key-jwt`; seamless sessions (15-min access token, silent rotating refresh with reuse detection, 8h idle / 12h absolute). `W0-P5` is unblocked.
  - **Original status (25 Sep):** note written, awaiting the owner's decision. Note: `docs/build-plan/w0-p4-portal-identity.md`; no code. **Found while surveying:** the portal already performs identity-bearing acts with invented identities. Every Propose records author `'portal'` (`app-chrome.tsx:33`), `author` is a caller-supplied server-action input, and Approve writes a hard-coded `meera.rao@example.com` into client state. Also, no `forge validate` rule stops an approval record approving itself. **Recommendation:** sign-in through the gateway (`POST /auth/local/token` at Wave 0, PKCE at Wave 1), token in a portal-server httpOnly cookie, author always from the session, and four server-side action gates. Proposer ≠ approver in three places (the gateway already; the portal; a new `approval-not-self-approved` rule). Personas come from a `personas:` block in the git mapping, and `portal-local` moves to `private-key-jwt`. Six owner decisions are listed in the note's §8.
  - **Found while starting `W0-P3`:** P3 cannot be built before this note is decided and `W0-P5` lands. W0-P2 §7 requires every `/api/v1/**` read to carry a resolved human, and the portal has none.

- [x] **W0-P5a** — The gateway as the local provider's token endpoint: sign-in, silent renewal, sign-out
  - model: opus
  - deps: W0-P4
  - wave: 0
  - reads: w0-p4-portal-identity.md §2, §9 decision 6; 02#4.4
  - touches: core/gateway/identity/**, core/gateway/store/**, core/gateway/transport/**, core/gateway/launch.ts
  - done: `POST /auth/local/token` (username + password + TOTP when enrolled) returns a 15-minute access token, a rotating refresh token and the principal; `POST /auth/local/refresh` rotates; `POST /auth/local/signout` ends the session; refresh tokens are stored only as a hash; reuse of a spent token revokes the whole session; 8 h idle / 12 h absolute; a disabled account stops renewing at the next refresh; the signed-in token alone opens no `/mcp` session; every refusal carries a `next`.
  - **Status: done 27 Sep 2026, branch `forge/W0-P5a-local-sign-in`.** `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS`: `core/gateway/identity/**`.
    - **Store:** two new tables in the one schema (`auth_session`, `auth_refresh_token`; migration 0011 in both dialects), plus `RuntimeStore.authSessions` (`store/identity/auth-session.ts`). The repository owns the rotation state machine. A spend is `update … set spent_at, replaced_by = <successor id> where spent_at is null`, followed by a re-read: whoever's id is on the row won. That keeps the check independent of driver row counts, like `approvals.expireDue`. Reuse, idle and absolute expiry each revoke the session in the same transaction. The first revoke reason is kept. The only token column is `token_hash`, and a test pins that.
    - **Identity:** `identity/local/sign-in.ts` `localSignInService` composes `LocalUserStore.authenticate` → `issueToken` → `authSessions`. `issueToken` gained an optional `authTime`, so a renewed token keeps the sign-in's `auth_time` rather than claiming a fresh authentication. Every renewal failure is **one** `AUTH_REQUIRED`: telling a thief "reused" would tell them they were noticed. A disabled account surfaces `issueToken`'s own `IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED` and revokes the session (`account_unavailable`).
    - **Transport:** `transport/sign-in-routes.ts`, mounted only when `localSignIn` is passed (launch passes it). It accepts only `application/json` (no cross-site form post; any script needs a preflight, which is never answered), caps the body at 16 KiB, sends `Cache-Control: no-store`, and turns any unexpected failure into a generic `INTERNAL` without its message. `sessionId` stays server-side. **Not a REST facade:** these routes serve no discovery, invocation or governance data.
    - **Session limits** default to 8 h / 12 h and are overridable through `LaunchOptions.sessionLimits`. **Their overlay home arrives with W0-P23's `identity.providers` block.** I did not invent an overlay file format here.
    - **Not in this task, stated:** sign-in and sign-out events are not written to the audit trail (the note does not ask for it, and `audit_call` is shaped for tool calls). There is no sweep of expired session rows yet; that is retention, like the nonce table's.
  - **Verified:** `store/identity/auth-session.test.ts` 9/9 (on SQLite the "concurrent" renewal pair is serialised, so it proves the one-winner outcome rather than racing the `replaced_by` claim; that race is Postgres-shaped). `identity/local/sign-in.test.ts` 11/11: real Argon2id store, real HS256 issuer, injected clock at the 8 h and 12 h edges; the refresh and access token values appear in no file under the store directory. `launch.e2e.test.ts` 8/8, with 4 new tests:
    - sign-in over HTTP gives a token that is the human on a real `/mcp` session with the registered test consumer;
    - the same token with no consumer is `CONSUMER_UNREGISTERED`;

Stage 8 (the privilege-escalation suite, "every escalation attempt must FAIL CLOSED") **fails** — and it is not a bug, it is a **deliberate tripwire firing exactly as designed**. `tests/policy/escalation.trust-boundary.test.ts:218` asserts that no TypeScript binding executor exists under `adapters/`, carrying its own message as the instruction: *"A TypeScript binding executor now exists under adapters/. Wave 0 exit criterion 5 needs a LIVE egress-refusal job against it (02 §4.8) — this suite currently evidences the ingress door only."* `adapters/function/**` now exists (7 files: `index.ts`, `mapping.ts`, `schema.ts`, `types.ts`, `testing/**`, `vitest.config.ts`), so the wire is tripped. **This matters because all six Wave 0 write tools are `function` bindings** — the adapter the tripwire names is the one every write goes through. Exit criterion 5 is "Gateway is the only door — direct module-server call refused", and the criteria table pairs it with criterion 14 as *"criterion 5 evidences the egress door; criterion 14 evidences the ingress door. Both are required — 01 §11.5."* Criterion 14 is satisfied (`W0-N14`); criterion 5's egress half is what this tripwire says is missing.
  - done: a live egress-refusal job proving a direct call to the `function` binding executor — bypassing the gateway's policy chain — is refused, not merely unreachable by convention; the tripwire assertion in `escalation.trust-boundary.test.ts` replaced by that real test rather than deleted or loosened; `pnpm test:policy` green with **every escalation attempt still failing closed**; exit criterion 5's row in the table below updated to name this task alongside `W0-E8`/`W0-N2`.
  - note: `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS` (`adapters/**/binding*`, `core/gateway/policy/**`). Do not satisfy this by relaxing the assertion — the tripwire was written by someone who foresaw exactly this moment.
  - **Status: done 25 Sep 2026, branch `forge/W0-P9-egress-refusal`. The owner chose the path: "execution grant + waiver".** The `function` adapter has **no real network client** (`AisClient` has only the in-process fake) and there is no JDE instance, so 02 §4.8's *network* control (the target accepts only the gateway's egress identity) cannot be evidenced at Wave 0. A mock target that refuses would prove only the mock, which the tripwire's author warned against. What *was* true: any in-process code could call `createFunctionExecutor` and dispatch **without the policy chain**, so the executor was unreachable only by convention.
    - **The execution grant** (`core/gateway/policy/execution-grant/`): at `proceed`, and only there, `runPolicyChain` mints a 30-second HMAC-SHA256 grant bound to **tool id, binding ref, business-argument hash (the confirm path's own `argsCanonicalHash`, excluding `confirm`), caller subject and correlation id**, with the consumer id carried for forensics. It uses a keyring of the confirm token's shape (dual-key rotation) but **a separate key** (`secretRef://gateway/execution-grant/hmac`), and the signature is domain-separated, so a confirm token can never pass as a grant. A mint that cannot bind is an `INTERNAL` **denial**, never a grant-less proceed. `PolicyRuntime.executionGrantKeyring` is optional in the type only: with none, `proceed` carries `executionGrant: null`, which every executor refuses (fail-closed at dispatch).
    - **The executor** (`adapters/function`): `createFunctionExecutor` now **requires** `grants: ExecutionGrantCheck` (no default; it throws if absent), and checks the grant as **step 0**, before validation, admission or dispatch. It refuses with `INTERNAL`, with a `next` naming `tools/call`/`forge.invoke`; no new error code was added. Binding the grant to the **ref** means an execute grant cannot drive the dry run's `_VALIDATE` sibling (the same executor) and a dry-run grant cannot drive the real orchestration.
    - **The live egress-refusal job** is `tests/policy/escalation.egress-grant.test.ts` (8 tests): the real chain, the real executor, the real HMAC check, the **real** `manifests/jde/fin/ap/voucher.create.tool.yaml` and its generated schema. The mock target serves only as the recorder of what reached it. The baseline dispatches once through the chain. A direct call with no grant, a grant minted with an attacker's key, a real grant replayed with different arguments / human / call, an execute grant used for the dry run, and a keyring-less chain are each **refused with zero dispatches**.
    - **The tripwire was replaced, not loosened** (`escalation.trust-boundary.test.ts`). Its purpose survives: any NEW TypeScript executor package under `adapters/` fails the suite until it enforces the grant and has its own egress job (the `GRANT_ENFORCING` map). A new static rule adds that `mintExecutionGrant` is called only under `core/gateway/policy/**`, that `createFunctionExecutor` is constructed nowhere in production (the allowlist is empty; **W0-P11 adds the gateway assembly and only that**), and that no production file imports the adapter's test doubles. **Mutation-checked:** a planted file breaking all three failed the suite; it was deleted.
    - **Known and bounded, not covered by the grant:** the capability probe (`core/probe/src/run/function-executor.ts`, 02 §4.5) dispatches over `AisClient` directly, by design. It is an operator action as a designated test identity, not an agent call, and it can only ever dispatch `MCPFORGE_PROBE_WHOAMI`, `<ref>_VALIDATE` and `MCPFORGE_PROBE_ORCHESTRATION_INFO`, never `ref` itself (its own structural test). Recorded here so nobody mistakes the grant for "the only path to a target".
    - **Also found:** `tests/policy/escalation.stored-credential.test.ts` holds a **second** tripwire that was already firing, masked in the CI output behind this one (stage 8's output shows `[2/2]`). Now `W0-P12`. It is not caused by this task and is not loosened here.
  - **Verified:** `tests/policy/escalation.egress-grant.test.ts` 8/8; trust-boundary 6/6; `core/gateway/policy/execution-grant` 20/20 (grant 15, chain-mint 5, including `forge.invoke` minting the identical grant); `adapters/function` 66/66 (5 new); `core/gateway` full suite 1093 passed / 16 skipped; `pnpm typecheck` clean across every workspace; `eslint .` clean. **`pnpm test:policy` → 101/102**: the one failure is the pre-existing W0-P12 tripwire, so stage 8 stays red until W0-P12.
  - **WAIVER, exit criterion 5, network-level egress half.** Accepted by the owner (Bikash Pattnaik) on 25 Sep 2026 by choosing this path. **Until:** a reachable JDE AIS instance exists (01 §4's Wave 0 prerequisite), or the Wave 1 checkpoint, whichever comes first. **What is unproven:** that the target refuses a connection that does not carry the gateway's egress identity (02 §4.8 controls 1–2). **What is proven instead:** nothing in the gateway process dispatches through the `function` executor without a policy-chain grant bound to that exact call.

Exit criteria 1 and 7 are evidenced by test harnesses, not by the running product. W0-G4's `done:` ("all four are ordinary MCP tools, always resident") is true of the definitions and false of the served endpoint. W0-P3's `/api/v1/**` would serve audit data that no live call produces.
  - done: `launchGateway` serves the real surface. The four meta-tools are resident; `tools/list` is the session's role-scoped set under the ≤1,300-token budget; every `tools/call` and `forge.invoke` runs the real policy chain with the real `PolicyContext` (store, identity, scope, kill switch, caps, keyring); a `proceed` dispatches through the real write dispatcher and binding executor (the `function` executor over an `AisClient` chosen by overlay config, the in-process fake at Wave 0) and writes the audit row. The stub tool is removed from the served server (kept only as a test fixture if still needed). **An end-to-end test drives the launched gateway over HTTP as a registered consumer with a resolved human identity:** `forge.find` → `forge.describe` → a read tool → a write tool plan → confirm → execute → audit row present; an unregistered consumer and an unresolved human are both refused (the existing tests must still pass against the assembled server, not a skeleton); `pnpm test:policy` green; `W0-C6` still passes. **From W0-P9:** the assembly supplies `PolicyRuntime.executionGrantKeyring` (from `secretRef://gateway/execution-grant/hmac`, never the confirm key), passes `PolicyDecision.executionGrant` through to the executor, constructs the executor with `executionGrantCheck(keyring)`, and adds exactly its own file to `EXECUTOR_CONSTRUCTORS` in `tests/policy/escalation.trust-boundary.test.ts`. Nothing here is new mechanism; the work is composition. **Do not re-implement any stage while wiring it.** A second copy of a gate is how the tested one and the served one drift apart. Should precede `W0-P3` (live audit data needs live calls) and `W0-Q12` (the external-agent demo needs a real endpoint).

Codegen's per-tool `handler.generated.ts` carries its OWN inline confirm-token mint and audit calls (its comments: *"Track C stub — core/gateway/store/audit is not built yet"*) and calls `binding.custom.ts`, which is still `throw new Error('NOT_IMPLEMENTED')` for 4 of the 6 write tools. That is Path B, a second copy of stages 6g/[9], and it is **not served**. Stripping it from codegen is `W0-P18`. The same client points at a real AIS by changing one overlay URL.

This task is then the launch wiring and the end-to-end HTTP test. **Found during the survey, each owned by a step below:** no composition root turns `generated/tools/**` into catalogue entries (store-world's header says so, and it builds two entries by hand); stage 6d's argument validator is still a stub everywhere outside unit tests; the transport authenticates the **consumer** only, never the human (no bearer handling in `transport/http.ts`); the group→role mapping reader lives in the **CLI** (`core/cli/src/lib/group-role-mapping.ts`), which the gateway cannot depend on; `writeDispatcher` is **write-only**, so whether a read-path dispatcher with an audit row exists must be checked, and if not, named; `AisRequest` carries **no human identity**, so how the HTTP client authenticates to JDE as the mapped user is undecided (see `W0-P14`); `@mcpforge/adapter-function` is only a dev dependency of the gateway.

  - model: opus
