  - context: Part of `W0-P11`. There is no composition root that turns the committed artefacts into the runtime objects the policy chain needs. `tests/write-path/support/store-world.ts` builds `PolicyCatalogueEntry`, `WriteSafetyView` and `ReversalContract` for two tools by hand (with a module-load agreement check against `generated/tools/<id>/tool.ts`), and says so in its header. Stage 6d's `ArgumentValidator` is the always-valid stub everywhere outside adapter unit tests.
  - **Owner decision, 25 Sep 2026: source the catalogue from the manifests, through `forge validate`.** Surveyed before any code: `generated/tools/<id>/tool.ts`'s `toolRegistration` is **incomplete** for runtime use. It has no `planTemplate`, confirm TTL, reversal `argMap`, `resultKeys`, server id, input mapping, execution caps or `echoOn`, which is why store-world reads the manifests with codegen's `readTool`. `@mcpforge/shared` has manifest TYPES only; parsing and validation live in `@mcpforge/codegen` (which depends only on `registry` and `shared`, so there is no cycle). Chosen: at startup the gateway loads `manifests/` through codegen's own reader and runs the same `forge validate` rules, **failing closed** on any error, then cross-checks every tool against its generated registration and `schema.json` so drift refuses to start. One parser, no codegen output change; the gateway gains a runtime dependency on `@mcpforge/codegen` (already in the single core image). Rejected: extending codegen's registration format (regenerates all 11 tools and changes a file format).
  - done: one module under `core/gateway/assembly/` that resolves, **for all 11 tools**, from `manifests/` validated with `forge validate`'s own rules (any failure refuses to start, naming the rule) and cross-checked against the committed `generated/` registration and schema (drift refuses to start): the `PolicyCatalogueEntry`, the `WriteSafetyView`, the reversal registry, the `function` binding descriptor and a compiled-Ajv `ArgumentValidator` over `generated/tools/<id>/schema.json` (never a second hand-written validator); a test asserting agreement with every manifest field store-world checks today, for all 11; drift between a generated artefact and its manifest fails at load, as store-world does; store-world switched to the resolver and its two hand builders deleted.
  - **Status: done 25 Sep 2026, branch `forge/W0-P13-catalogue-resolver`.** New `core/gateway/assembly/` (`catalogue.ts`, `index.ts`, exported as `@mcpforge/gateway/assembly`). `loadRuntimeCatalogue({repoRoot})` (1) runs `validateRepo` with its default rules, the identical call `forge validate` makes, so any failure refuses to start and names forge validate's own rule id; (2) reads every Tool manifest with codegen's `readTool`; (3) cross-checks each tool against its committed artefacts. It checks the `manifest-sha256` header of `tool.ts` and `schema.json` against the manifest's bytes, `schema.json` against `buildSchemaJson` output for today's manifest, and the registration's fields against the manifest view: store-world's fields for **every** tool, plus `governance.policyException`. It also refuses on a missing generated tree, an orphan generated tree, and a `reversal.tool` that did not resolve. Rule ids are `assembly.*`, and all failures are reported together in one `CatalogueLoadRefused`. (4) It then builds the frozen `PolicyCatalogueEntry`, `WriteSafetyView`, the reversal registry, the `function` descriptor (the adapter's own `buildFunctionBindingDescriptor`), and the 6d `ArgumentValidator`, which is compiled Ajv over the **committed** `schema.json` via the adapter's own `compileGeneratedSchema`. The validator fails closed for an unknown tool or a call/entry mismatch, and `schemaValidatorFor(id)` hands the executor the **same** compiled function. Nothing defaults a missing write-safety value; it is refused as `assembly.resolve`. The gateway's `@mcpforge/adapter-function` dependency moved from dev to runtime, and `@mcpforge/codegen` was added, per the owner decision. **One deliberate difference from the deleted builders:** the reversal contract now also carries `windowHours` and `preconditions` from the manifest; the old builder dropped them, so `forge audit reverse`'s window check had nothing to read in that world. **store-world** now takes the entries, write-safety views, reversal registry and **the real 6d validator** from the resolver. `catalogueEntryFor`, `writeSafetyViewFor`, `reversalContractFor`, `manifestView` and the two-tool `assertGeneratedAgrees` are deleted, and `assertPosture` stays. **Verified:** `core/gateway/assembly/catalogue.test.ts` has 56 tests: agreement for all 11 tools against an independent plain-YAML read of each manifest, 6d behaviour, and six refusal cases on a temp repo copy (forge-validate failure, manifest-sha256, schema-drift, registration-drift, generated-missing, generated-orphan). Gateway suite 1149 passed / 16 skipped, 77/77 files. Write-path 8/8 (was 8/8, now with the real 6d). `pnpm test:policy` 101/102: the one failure is the known `W0-P12` tripwire, unchanged. Gateway `tsc` and eslint are clean, and `forge codegen` is byte-identical. **Pre-existing failures found, filed as `W0-P20`, not caused here** (they fail identically on `main` at `114708c`). **Flag for the owner:** `core/gateway/meta/types.ts`'s header still says *"THE SEAMS BELOW EXIST BECAUSE THE GATEWAY MAY NOT IMPORT CODEGEN"*. Your 25 Sep decision supersedes that for the assembly. The comment was left untouched because it is outside this task's `touches`, and the meta layer's loaded-artefact seams are still sound; its wording should be updated when `W0-P16` wires the meta layer.

  - model: opus

  - context: Part of `W0-P11`; owner decision of 25 Sep 2026. `AisClient` has only the in-process fake, so the live gateway has nothing to talk to, and W0-P9's static rule forbids production code importing test doubles. **The blast-radius question this task must answer first:** `AisRequest` carries no human identity. 02 §3.5 requires the orchestration to run **as the mapped JDE user** (identity carriage, echo-checked), so the HTTP client must authenticate per user, and non-negotiables #1 and #8 decide how. **If 02 §3.5 does not specify the per-user AIS authentication, stop and name the gap: that is a `needs_human`, not a shared service token.**
  - done: an `AisClient` over HTTP (the AIS orchestration REST shape, timeout via the abort signal, target errors mapped as the fake maps them), authenticating as the caller's mapped JDE identity exactly as 02 §3.5 specifies and never with a shared credential; the mock AIS served as a standalone local HTTP process (outside production code) with a documented start command; the overlay chooses the base URL; contract tests run the executor through the HTTP client against the local mock; the W0-P9 egress job still passes.
  - **Status: done 25 Sep 2026, branch `forge/W0-P14-ais-http-client`.** **The blast-radius question, answered from the spec rather than decided:** 02 §3.5 specifies option (a), where the gateway exchanges the caller's identity for a per-user AIS token via the instance's trusted token provider. 05 §382 lists the "JDE token-provider client credential" in Wave 0's stored set, and 02 §11.5.1 names the class `per-user-exchanged`. So the gateway authenticates to the token provider **as itself**, with one `secretRef://binding/<serverId>/…` client credential per module server (02 §11.5 rule 4). That credential can only **ask** for a per-user token for `Principal.subject`; it is never sent to an orchestration, and every orchestration runs under the per-user token, which the runtime echo then proves. **No shared AIS login exists anywhere.** Built:
    - `AisRequest.principalSubject`, copied by the executor from the session and never from an argument.
    - `adapters/function/src/http/`: `createHttpAisClient` (`POST <baseUrl>/v3/orchestrator/<name>` with a per-user `jde-AIS-Auth` header, the pinned orchestration version and the correlation id), `createHttpAisTokenProvider` (tokens cached **per subject**; the client secret is read through a structural `secretStore.get(ref)` inside `adapters/**`, which rule 2 allows; values are never logged), and a strict `AisTargets` overlay parser. No constructor option exists for a static token, a service user or a default subject.
    - Mapping: no subject, or a subject the provider does not know (403/404), gives `IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED` with nothing sent. A rejected gateway credential (401) or an unreachable provider gives `TARGET_UNAVAILABLE`, with a `next` naming `forge secrets status`. An orchestration 401 drops the cached token and gives `IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED`, and the next call re-exchanges. 400/409/412/422 is `TARGET_PRECONDITION_FAILED` and any other non-2xx is `TARGET_ERROR`, via `targetError` exactly as the fake reports it. Abort gives `TARGET_TIMEOUT`.
    - `overlays/local/ais-targets.yaml` points all three servers at the mock, each with its own ref. `aisTargetForServer` refuses an unlisted server; there is no default target.
    - The mock JDE is `tests/mocks/src/mock-jde/server.ts`, a real HTTP server with a token provider and orchestrations. It executes as the token's user and composes the `MCPFORGE_PROBE_WHOAMI` echo step. Standalone start: **`pnpm --filter @mcpforge/mocks mock-jde`** (127.0.0.1:4545, `--port`, `--config`). Provisioning is in `tests/mocks/fixtures/mock-jde/local.yaml` (users and client ids, **no secrets**). Client secrets are generated into the git-ignored `.mcpforge/mock-jde/clients.json`, and only that path is printed.
  - **Verified:** `tests/mocks/src/mock-jde/http-contract.test.ts`, 14 tests through the real executor with shipped manifests and schemas: a write runs as the caller with the echo matched, only mapped inputs are sent, tokens are per subject, expiry re-exchanges, no-subject / unknown-subject / bad-client-credential are all refused with nothing run, a misconfigured instance is caught by the echo, and **no execution grant means zero requests reach the mock**. Also the error mappings, a timeout, an unreachable target, the local overlay, and **the standalone process spawned and called**. A mutation check (disabling token invalidation) fails the expiry test. Adapter tests 73/73 (including 7 new overlay tests), mocks 29/29, probe 88/88, gateway 1149/1149, `pnpm test:policy` 101/102 (the known `W0-P12` tripwire; the W0-P9 egress job passes), overlay-purity clean, and typecheck (`tsc --build`) and eslint clean. The two new error builders were appended at the end of `adapters/function/src/errors.ts` because `core/gateway/errors/enumeration.test.ts` pins existing `next` sites by line number.
  - **Named follow-ups, not silently decided:**
    - **(1) The token-exchange wire protocol is MCPForge's own, documented in `token-provider.ts`.** 02 §3.5 names the mechanism, not the format, and there is no JDE instance to read it from. Matching a real JDE token provider's protocol behind the same `AisTokenProvider` seam is a `needs_human` for when an instance exists; the stand-in until then is the mock, never a shared token.
    - **(2) `overlay-purity`'s declared-key check (`tools/ci`, outside this task) does not yet know `kind: AisTargets`.** The file passes as an unrecognised kind; its content scan does run and passes. The adapter's parser is strict in the meantime.
    - **(3) For `W0-P11`:** seed the gateway's SecretStore refs `secretRef://binding/<serverId>/token-provider-client` from the mock's `clients.json`, and build one client per server via `aisTargetForServer`.
    - **(4) Identity model, as the existing echo already assumes:** the JDE user **is** the caller's subject, compared case-insensitively, and the mock's `users` list is the provisioning.

    - **`establish()`** runs at `initialize`, only after `[2a]`. It refuses a consumer with no compiled authorization (`CONSUMER_UNREGISTERED`) or a non-active one (`CONSUMER_SUSPENDED`). It then authenticates the human through the injected `IdentityProvider` and maps groups plus `subjectOverrides` to roles. It returns the real `ScopeContext` (`scopeAt(now)`) with the consumer's frozen provenance (W0-N10), whose `consumerSessionId` is the MCP session id, now minted before the SDK sees the request.
    - **`reverify()`** runs on **every later request** (05 §11.8: per-user identity on every call). The bearer is verified again, and a different subject on the same session id is refused `AUTH_REQUIRED`, so a session id is never an identity. The human's roles are re-derived from the fresh token, so a group removal takes effect on the next call.
    - **Transport** (`transport/http.ts` + `session-binding.ts`): a generic `SessionEstablisher` seam. A refusal at `initialize` leaves no McpServer, no transport and no session row. Every request on a live session is re-verified before the MCP SDK sees it. The server factory receives a `SessionHandle` with the latest verified session, for `W0-P16`. `close()` now drops idle keep-alive sockets, which also fixed a shutdown stall.
    - **Mapping reader moved, not duplicated.** `core/cli/src/lib/group-role-mapping.ts` → `core/gateway/identity/group-role-mapping.ts`, with its test. The CLI file is now a one-line re-export, and `forge identity remap` runs the same code. New `loadDeploymentGroupRoleMapping`: it fails closed on parse errors, a file claiming another deployment, or no mapping at all. New `rolesForPrincipal` uses own-property lookups only.
    - **`overlays/local/deployment.yaml`** (`kind: Deployment`, `packages: [jde-fin]`), with a strict parser in `assembly/deployment.ts`.
    - **Target-identity mappings:** none exist for a Wave 0 binding type. JDE identity is carried by W0-P14's per-user token exchange; the db- and ebs-identity mappings arrive with those binding types. This is stated in the module rather than faked with a loader that reads nothing.
  - **Verified:**
    - `assembly/session.test.ts` (19 tests): the real transport, real `ConsumerAuthenticator`, real local `IdentityProvider` and real committed artefacts. It covers both parties present, no bearer (`AUTH_REQUIRED`, no session, no server built), a verified token for a deactivated user (`IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED`), an unregistered consumer (the human is never resolved), no compiled authorization, and a suspended registration. It proves the **intersection**: a read-only consumer hides every write, a no-role human sees nothing, and consumer roles that miss the human's role show nothing. It covers re-verification (same human served; no bearer or another person's token refused before the SDK) and fail-closed startup.
    - `tests/policy/escalation.session.test.ts` (6 tests): the consumer and identity escalation cases against the **real session**, including case 5, a forged-issuer token, the write intersection (`CONSUMER_NOT_AUTHORIZED`) and case 6 (a consumer kill mid-session empties the live session's scope).
    - **Mutation check:** disabling the same-subject rule fails the takeover test.
    - Session tests are 10/10 green on repeat runs, after a flake from per-test catalogue reloads and keep-alive shutdown was fixed.
    - Gateway 1185 passed / 16 skipped (79/79 files). `pnpm test:policy` 107/108 (the known `W0-P12` tripwire). CLI identity 7/7, consumer-access 6/6. Gateway and CLI `tsc --build` clean, eslint clean, overlay-purity clean.

(3) `dev.test.ts`: a snapshot of what the suite could touch in the real repo (the `portal-local` record's bytes, the `consumers/` and `approvals/` listings, the verifier file) is taken at module load and must be unchanged at the end. `pnpm -C core/cli exec vitest run`: **133/133** in isolation.

Non-negotiable 8 allows `SecretStore.get()` only in `adapters/**` and `core/gateway/identity/**`, so the portal cannot hold a client secret. **Human gate:** the agent stages the consumer-record proposal and the owner approves and merges it; the agent never fills the approval record.

  - **APPROVED AND MERGED 28 Sep 2026 by the owner.** The owner's words: *"Approve it (Recommended)"*, given in session after being asked explicitly. The agent recorded it at the owner's instruction: `approver: Bikash Pattnaik`, `approvedAt: 2026-09-28`, `selfApproved: true` (W0-P4 decision 3: an admin may approve their own definitional change). The authorizations are unchanged; only the credential method and public key changed. `forge validate` exits 0 and codegen changed only the source hash in `generated/consumers/portal-local.authorization.json`. **Live check:** the real gateway on :3939, sent an assertion signed by `core/portal/src/lib/gateway-client/consumer-assertion.ts` with the real key file, answered `401 AUTH_REQUIRED`: the consumer was accepted and the human is required next. With no credential it answered `CONSUMER_UNREGISTERED`. The owner also chose to leave the stale `test-agent-1` proposal staged ("Leave it staged (Recommended)").
  - **Status: agent's part done 27 Sep 2026, branch `forge/W0-P24-portal-private-key-jwt`.** `OPUS_GUARDED_PATHS`: none of the code touched is guarded. The staged proposal targets `consumers/**` (guarded), and the owner reviews it.
    - **Order, and why it differs from the `done:` wording:** the key is minted *before* approval, not after. `consumerRecordSchema` rejects a `private-key-jwt` record with no `credential.publicKeys[]` (05 §A.5), so a proposal that only changed the method would fail `forge validate` and could never authenticate. 05 §A.4 already orders it this way: step 2 issues (the public key goes into the record), then step 3 approves. The method switch and the public key therefore arrive in **one** reviewed diff.
    - **CLI:** `forge consumer issue-credential <id> --method private-key-jwt [--key-file <path>]` (default stays `client-secret`, so the existing behaviour is unchanged). It mints an Ed25519 keypair (`core/gateway/consumer/keypair.ts`, `node:crypto`). The **private** key goes to `--key-file`, which is refused outside `.mcpforge/` and refused over an existing file, or else it is printed once. The **public** key goes only into a staged proposal (`proposePrivateKeyJwtKey` in `lifecycle.ts`, action `rotate-credential-schedule`). A `client-secret` record switches method with this key as its only key. A `private-key-jwt` record keeps its old keys beside the new one for the rotation overlap. `--key-file` together with `client-secret`, and `mtls`, are refused, each with a `next`. The CI and staging/prod refusals run before anything is minted. Key file format: `{consumerId, kid, alg: EdDSA, createdAt, privateJwk}`. `kid` is `<issue date>-<4 hex>`.
    - **Portal:** `core/portal/src/lib/gateway-client/consumer-assertion.ts` reads `.mcpforge/portal/portal-local.private.jwk.json` (override: `MCPFORGE_PORTAL_CONSUMER_KEY_FILE`) and signs a single-use assertion per call: `iss` = `sub` = `portal-local`, `aud` = `MCPFORGE_GATEWAY_AUDIENCE` (default `https://mcpforge.local/mcp`), a fresh `jti`, `exp` = `iat` + 60, and the key's `kid`. It sends the assertion in the `mcpforge-consumer-assertion` header. It imports nothing from the gateway at runtime and holds no `SecretStore`. A missing or foreign key raises `ConsumerCredentialError` (`CONSUMER_KEY_MISSING` / `CONSUMER_KEY_INVALID`), whose `next` names the issue command, and key material is never echoed. `jose` is added as a portal dependency. **W0-P3 wires `consumerAssertionHeaders()` beside the viewer's bearer token.**
    - **Verified:** `consumer-assertion.test.ts` passes 8/8 against the gateway's **real** `ConsumerAuthenticator`. The committed `portal-local` record with the key proposal applied authenticates as `private-key-jwt`. The same assertion against today's committed `client-secret` record is refused `CONSUMER_UNREGISTERED`. Replays and wrong-audience assertions are refused. For this, `tools/ci/src/portal-http-boundary.ts` gained **test-only** allowances (`consumer`: the mint/propose/write functions; `transport`: the authenticator, the presentation and the header constant), with a justification. CLI `consumer.test.ts`: 26/26 (7 new). Gateway consumer, consumer-auth and secrets: 165/165. Portal: 813/813. `forge validate` exits 0, the regen-diff is clean, and `pnpm test:policy` is 107/108 (the known W0-P12 tripwire). The CLI suite has 4 failures, and all 4 fail identically on `main`: 3 are **W0-P20**'s stale tests, and 1 is the spawned-binary timeout family.
    - **Staged:** `.mcpforge/proposals/2026-09-27-consumer-rotate-portal-local/`. It moves `portal-local` to `private-key-jwt` with public key `2026-09-27-06e2`. The approval `approvals/2026-09-27-consumer-portal-local-rotate-credential-schedule.yaml` is `pending`, with approver and date blank, and `requestedBy` is the owner. The private key is at `.mcpforge/portal/portal-local.private.jwk.json` (git-ignored). **On Windows the `0600` mode is not enforced by NTFS:** the file is protected only by the user profile's ACL.
    - **Consequence to know:** `rm -rf .mcpforge/` deletes the portal's key. The portal then refuses with `CONSUMER_KEY_MISSING` until a new key is issued and its proposal approved. It fails closed, not open. Found in passing: the 19 Sep staged `test-agent-1` proposal is `private-key-jwt` with **no public key**, so it fails `forge validate` as it stands. **Gap, not closed here:** `issue-credential` looks up only *registered* consumers, so it cannot add a key to a registration that is still only staged. 05 §A.4's new → issue → approve sequence for a brand-new `private-key-jwt` consumer therefore still has no CLI path. It needs `forge consumer new --method private-key-jwt --key-file …` (or equivalent) to mint the key into the registration proposal. That is a follow-up task, and it is outside P24's `touches`.

## Track Q — Prototype-parity gaps found by the 25 Sep 2026 independent assessment (12 tasks)

Added 25 Sep 2026. The owner asked for an independent check of whether every **platform** capability the concept console showed has been factored into the build: Registry, Developer Workspace, Business Intake, Governance & Approvals, Consumption Graph and Application Enablement. The concept console is `mcpforge-console.html` in the owner's Downloads folder (18 Aug), and a later copy is `..\mcpforge-console_1.html`. **Do not read either at runtime.** This preamble and each task below restate what is needed. 03 §5.1 deliberately maps all six areas to portal routes, and the narrative pages (Overview, Why, Walkthrough, Plan) were removed on purpose. **This track is about what fell out between that map and the build**, and it has three root causes:

1. **Routes 03 §5.2 specifies that were never built:** `/catalog/servers/[serverId]`, `/requests/[requestId]` and `/activity/consumption`. No J-track task named them, so no gate caught their absence.
2. **Console capabilities that no build-plan document carries at all.** The main one is **model-assisted authoring**: the console's "describe the tool you need, then Generate tool spec", and its six-stage pipeline in which *"the model writes descriptions, examples, error maps and eval utterances; deterministic codegen writes the plumbing."* **Harvest** and **Normalize** (pulling an application's raw API surface into a capability inventory mapped onto entity and verb) are also missing. No component in the repo calls a model. For an AI product this is the largest gap between the console and the build, and because it was never decided, `W0-Q8` starts with a design note.
3. **Two CLI commands still on the W0-A5 skeleton:** `forge new tool` (02 §2.5's authoring step, and the lane's automation seam at 02 §8) and `forge test`. Both print "not implemented yet".

**Already planned, so not re-tasked here:** duplicate detection with a merge-or-justify decision (roadmap G1 @ M1, Wave 1; Wave 0 search is lexical only); the anomaly-monitoring *product* (Wave 3; the Wave 0 groundwork exists); the server inventory (`W0-P6`); making the fixture pages live (`W0-P3`).

**Lesson carried from Track P, applied to every `done:` below:** each one requires the feature to be **reachable from a real entry point and fed by real data**. Rendering and passing axe is not enough.

- [ ] **W0-Q1** — The Consumption Graph: `/activity/consumption`
  - model: sonnet
  - deps: W0-P3
  - wave: 0
  - reads: 03#5.2, 01#2 (G9), 01#11, 02#11.3
  - touches: core/portal/src/app/activity/consumption/**
  - context: The console's Consumption Graph promised *"for every MCP server and tool, know every registered agent that calls it"*. 03 §5.2 specifies the route and roadmap G9 @ M1 makes it an exit criterion (*"every tool → consuming agent, platform, scope, call count"*), but no task built it. The data already exists: since Phase 5, every audit row carries an authenticated `consumer_id`, which is what makes G9 provable (01 §11). The closest existing surface is the per-consumer usage panel in `/activity/consumers`, and that one is organised by consumer, not by tool.
  - done: `/activity/consumption` renders tool → consumer edges with consumer class, the scope each consumer was granted, call count and last call for a chosen window, all read from W0-P3's `/api/v1/**` seam (never a fixture); it can pivot from tool to consumers and from consumer to tools; a tool with zero consumers is shown as such, not hidden, because that is G9's sprawl signal; it is reachable from the Activity nav and from a tool's Catalog drawer; the gateway-down state from W0-P3 applies; the a11y gates (stages 14–17) pass.

It touches four settled commitments at once, so it needs owner decisions, not agent ones: **local-first** (§3.1: no cloud account may become a prerequisite, so the feature must be optional and the build must work fully without it); **secrets** (a provider API key is a credential, so it must be a `secretRef://` and pass non-negotiable #8); **data egress** (a draft manifest names systems, modules and business rules that leave the machine); and **steward-authored evals** (CLAUDE.md §4: `evals/` is authored by module stewards, *not tool authors*, so a model may suggest intents but must never be the author of record).
  - done: a design note, reviewed by the owner before any code, deciding: (a) exactly which manifest fields a model may draft, at minimum `purpose`, `disambiguation`, `planTemplate`, parameter `desc` and error `next` copy, and which it may **never** write: bindings, `writeSafety` values, `identity.*`, grants, `reviewPath`, and anything in `generated/`; (b) the provider seam, meaning whether this is a pluggable interface like `IdentityProvider`/`ChangeHost`/`SecretStore` and which provider and model is the default; (c) key handling as a `secretRef://` and where it may be dereferenced under `no-secret-value-escape`; (d) what leaves the machine, and whether overlays can turn the feature off per deployment; (e) how drafted copy is gated: `forge validate`, the codegen token budgets, and a human accepting each field, with provenance recorded on the proposal; (f) how suggested eval intents stay steward-owned; (g) behaviour with no key configured: the feature is absent, not broken, and nothing else depends on it.

## Wave 0 exit criteria — the map from tasks to criteria

The lane's checkpoint generator uses this table to pre-fill agenda item 1. **A criterion with no passing task is a fail, and a fail blocks PROCEED without a written waiver naming who accepted the risk and until when.**

| # | Wave 0 exit criterion (01 §7) | Satisfied by |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 11 tools live across 3 servers, manifest as sole source of truth | W0-I1 … W0-I6 |
| 2 | Contract tests 100%; regeneration produces zero differences | W0-B7, W0-B9 |
| 3 | Probe reports zero silent failures; every binding resolved or visibly disabled | W0-H4 |
| 4 | Identity carriage **verified, not assumed**, for every `function` binding | W0-H5, W0-H3 |
| 5 | Gateway is the only door — direct module-server call refused | W0-E8 **+ `W0-N2`** + **`W0-P9`** (in-code egress: the execution grant) *(criterion 5 evidences the egress door; criterion 14 evidences the ingress door. Both are required — 01 §11.5.)* **Network-level egress half: WAIVED until a JDE AIS instance exists or the Wave 1 checkpoint — see W0-P9's status.** |
| 6 | One P2P role spans all three servers and grants exactly its declared scope | W0-E2, W0-I1 |
| 7 | **Six write tools** through the full path, **plus** (a) live reversal, (b) refusal at confirm, (c) refusal at policy, (d) idempotent replay | W0-F1 … W0-F7, W0-I3, W0-I4, W0-I5 |
| 8 | Every tool has an approval record from its mandated review path | W0-HG6 |
| 9 | Portal is a UX over git; a portal-only edit is structurally impossible | W0-J12, W0-C6 |
| 10 | Benchmark suite exists and runs in CI; all five metrics; baseline recorded | W0-G6, W0-G7, W0-HG7 |
| 11 | 100% of error paths return an agent-actionable next step; zero dead ends | W0-E4, W0-B7 |
| 12 | EBS enablement track opened — ISG holder named, IREP inventory started, wrapper schema requested | *(non-code, human — track it at the checkpoint)* |
| 13 | Autonomy baseline recorded; every intervention logged and categorised | *(the lane's `interventions.csv`, automatic)* |
| 14 | **The front door is closed and elevated bindings are not open** — (a) unregistered refused, (b) suspended refused mid-session, (c) elevated binding refused through both entry points, (d) registration/standing-auth/rotation each with an approval record | `W0-N1` … `W0-N4`, `W0-N11`, `W0-N14`, `W0-HG8` |

**Criterion 7's audit-immutability wording carries a Wave 0 limitation** that must be recorded as an accepted waiver rather than passed silently: on SQLite, immutability is **detectable** (append-only triggers plus the hash chain), **not preventable**. It becomes preventable at the Postgres migration. See `02_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` §10.4 item 1.

| Track | Tasks | Opus | Sonnet | Human |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A Foundations | 7 | 2 | 5 | — |
| B Manifest + codegen | 9 | 4 | 5 | — |

| **P Audit-found completeness gaps (24 Sep 2026)** | **20** | **16** | **3** | **1** |
| **Q Prototype-parity gaps (25 Sep 2026)** | **12** | **4** | **6** | **2** |
| **Total** | **143** | **63** | **69** | **11** |

**43 Opus / 60 Sonnet / 8 human** — about **42%** of automated tasks on Opus, concentrated in tracks B, C, D, E, F, G, H and N, which are the security-sensitive two-thirds of the build. Track J is 19 Sonnet tasks and 2 Opus ones, and the two Opus ones (`W0-J12` change model, `W0-J18` roles editor) are governance mechanisms wearing a UI costume — which is exactly why routing the portal as a block would be a mistake. **The Opus share rose four points because Phase 5's additions sit almost entirely on the security spine: the consumer record and the anomaly-event schema fire Test 1, and every gate in track N fires Test 2. That is the correct place for the share to rise and the wrong place to economise.**
